# Hats

Registry of all hats, and of the ranges rented out to other files (below). Never reuse or renumber. Keep this file up to date. Types `operation` and `control` (^371, ^392): a `control` is one input (or its alternatives) in one context; an `operation` is defined in ^391. Type `operation` (^371): a feature the user can do; every operation is a feature, so list it as `operation` instead of `feature`. The controls that trigger it are in `docs/controls.md`, which only references hats.

| Hat | Type | Status | Summary |
|-----|------|--------|---------|
| ^1 | decision | decided | JS framework: **Svelte 5** (with Vite, on bun). Rendering of the map itself is Canvas 2D for now. |
| ^2 | feature | done (merged) | Infinite zoomable/pannable canvas with a 1 m x 2 m box in the center. Wheel/pinch zooms at cursor, drag pans. Scale 0.02 to 400 px/m, view center limited to ±2500 m. |
| ^3 | decision | decided | TypeScript pinned to 6.0.3 (not 5.9, which was an unverified guess; thanks Leo). TS 7.0 lacks the programmatic API typescript-eslint needs; revisit at 7.1 stable (planned 2026-11-24). |
| ^4 | decision | decided | World coordinate system: meters, origin at map center, **y down** (like the screen). |
| ^5 | demo | reference | "Through-node curves" demo (Figs G to J), saved as `standalone-demos/hat5.html`. Behavior reference for the port (^13). |
| ^6 | decision | decided | **Through-node curve model:** nodes lie *on* the curve and each carries a heading. An edge between two nodes is one of: *bend* (straight, arc, straight; the arc's circle is tangent to both heading lines), *S-curve* (straight, arc, straight through the inflection point, arc, straight; both arcs share one radius), *hook* (same-direction two-arc edge, up to ~285 degrees; provisional, not designed). A single arc cannot exceed 180 degrees in the bend form. Source: handover section 2. See ^7, ^8. |
| ^7 | decision | decided; the stored tag was dropped again in ^172 (the shape is derived) | **Shape tag:** positions + headings + radius do not fully determine an edge (same data can be a bend or an S; a hook has a rotation direction). So each edge stores a shape tag (bend / S / hook, plus first turn direction). It is chosen automatically at creation and cleared when a heading is edited. If a stored tag stops being valid the edge falls back to automatic choice. Refines ^6. |
| ^8 | decision | decided | **Radius:** one per edge, either *auto* (the largest that fits) or explicit. Changing the radius never changes headings. Bend gesture: the circle tangent to both heading lines through the cursor, grabbed at the arc midpoint. S/hook gesture: drag along the radial direction; both arcs of an S share the radius. Double-click resets to auto. Minimum radius 0.5 m in the demo (defaults per road class: see handover, unverified). Refines ^6. |
| ^9 | decision | decided | **Handle vocabulary:** black dot = node (position); black arrow with ring = heading (arrow tip drags it); blue point = where the two heading lines meet (bend), dragging sets both headings; on an S it is the generated inflection point; orange ring = radius; turquoise = corners of the control polygon (S: display only; hook: drag sets radius). Overlay colors: purple curve, blue guides, red labels. The handover's wider color legend (gray committed, green creating, magenta modifying, blue selection, red invalid) is not yet applied. Handles keep a constant on-screen size at any zoom. See ^6, ^8. |
| ^10 | decision | decided | **Insert a node on a curve:** the new node takes the tangent as its heading and both halves keep the radius and a matching shape tag, so the curve does not change. See ^6, ^7, ^8. |
| ^11 | decision | decided; the tools (M, A, I keys) were superseded by ^44 | **Editing conventions (demo):** tools Move / Append / Insert (keys M, A, I); Append extends at the nearer end of the path, new heading chosen so the new edge is a single bend; right-click or Del removes a node; Esc returns to Move; Shift while dragging a heading snapped to 15 degrees (removed, ^251); handles win over empty space, nodes win over other handles when the cursor is right on them. Handover conventions not yet ported: typed length/angle, 0.5 m click filter, guide snapping, one-road-one-undo. |
| ^12 | decision | decided | **Port architecture:** curve geometry is a pure, tested library in `src/lib/curves/` working in meters (the demo's 6 px = 1 m); the editor interaction engine is separate from the canvas component; the canvas component only dispatches input and draws. The demo stays as a reference. Builds on ^4. |
| ^13 | feature | done (merged) | Port the ^5 demo into the app on the pan/zoom canvas (^2): all three tools, handles, presets, readout, legend, dark mode. The ^2 test box is removed. Not ported: touch pinch zoom, undo. Behavior-preserving except: handles keep a constant screen size at any zoom (demo scaled them with the canvas), and hit areas are in screen pixels. |
| ^14 | decision | superseded by ^30 (touch part still holds) | **Navigation vs editing input:** wheel/pinch zooms; dragging empty space or middle-button drag pans; dragging a handle edits. In Append/Insert tools a click acts, and panning needs the middle button. Space+drag was removed (did not work). Touch: one-finger on empty space pans. |
| ^15 | bug | fixed | While dragging a handle, the blue hover ring stayed at the point where the drag started. Fix: clear the hover when a drag starts. Reported by the user while testing ^13. |
| ^16 | bug | fixed | Nodes 1 (heading up) and 2 (heading east), whose heading lines meet in a proper corner, became an S-curve instead of an L (single bend). Cause: automatic shape choice also required a two-arc approximation not to inflect, which misfires when the nodes are far apart along one heading. Fix: a bend is chosen whenever the heading lines meet properly (refines ^6, ^7); the inflection check now only decides S vs hook. Reported with a screenshot by the user. |
| ^17 | bug | fixed | Dragging the orange radius ring of an S-curve was inverted (toward the turquoise corner increased the radius). Cause: a linear radius-from-displacement rule with the wrong sign. Fix: for S and hook the radius is now the one whose arc midpoint is nearest the cursor, so the ring follows the cursor (as the bend gesture already did). Refines ^8. |
| ^18 | question | closed: moot since ^132 (draggable with a stored middle line) and ^134 (orange) | User observed that the turquoise points are draggable on the hook preset but not on Fig G (S-curve). By design (^9): on an exact S the corners lie on fixed lines and do not depend on the radius, so there is nothing to drag; on a hook they move with the radius, so dragging picks it. Proposal: draw display-only points differently (e.g. small hollow rings) so they do not look draggable. Awaiting user decision. |

## Roadmap

The roadmap (^146): items of type `feature`, `bug` or `chore` in the user's order of priority (user's first draft: many need more concrete requirements and design, shared between user and Claude). Status "planned" until the open questions are settled.

### Milestones (^147)

An open feature is in at most one planned milestone, and each milestone is a hat. A roadmap item can be unassigned until we plan it. Decisions, questions and bugs in brackets belong to the same set. The suggested order is Claude's; the user decides the priority.

Notes (Claude): an outline is two thin lines at the same distance on both sides of the pieces of the hovered edge (or of the branch) with a half circle at each end; the offset of a line is a line and the offset of an arc is an arc with another radius, so it is exact and needs no new geometry kind. |
| ^100 | decision | planned (user definition; made precise by ^334, ^254; milestone ^816, which reworks it with ^817 to ^819) | **Parallel segments and their centerline (user, from the todo list):** two segments A and B are *parallel* if (1) their start nodes have the same or exactly opposite heading and their end nodes have the same or exactly opposite heading (dot product 1 or -1), (2) the distance between their two start nodes and between their two end nodes is exactly `lanewidth(A)/2 + lanewidth(B)/2`, and (3) the centers of their arc radius circles are at the same point (concentric). Parallel segments share a centerline, rendered dashed, or solid if (1) the two segments head in opposite directions and (2) one of the two is also parallel to a third segment that heads the same way as itself, or the right segment heads backwards (which would indicate left-hand traffic; modelcity assumes right-hand traffic for now, this might change later). Notes (Claude): the snapping of ^94 only makes lanes approximately parallel (it fits each edge between snapped headings); this exact definition needs snapped nodes that are on the same circle, see ^101. |
| ^101 | feature | done (milestone ^153, PR #30) | **Snap lines and snap points (user, from the todo list):** snap *lines* (solid turquoise in dark mode) leave one degree of freedom, snap *points* (solid turquoise rings in dark mode) none. The distance to a snap point is the distance of two points; the distance to a snap line is the shortest distance between a point and the line. Snap suggestions are shown by proximity. Nodes snap along the normals of other nodes' headings when close to that line, and additionally there is a snap point where the two nodes would touch, or rather the single point where their respective imagined circles with the radius of the lane width intersect. Notes (Claude): this generalizes the parallel snapping of ^94 (removed in ^124, code in `f694dcd^`), which snapped to a line beside a curve; turquoise is free again since S-curve and hook gizmos are yellow (^85); snap guides are new gizmos, so they need a place in the skeleton/scaffold vocabulary (^60). | The radius snaps to the largest radius when dragged within 0.5% of it (^141); that snap is to be considered here, probably as a snap to the limiting node. |

| ^105 | decision | done (milestone ^149, the model swap PR; the hook corners are hollow rings, ^174) | **Hook control points are derived (user, from the todo list):** the positions of the two control points in hooks are derived from the data and are no longer controllable by dragging. | ^106 | feature | done (milestone ^149, the model swap PR; see ^174 for the details) | **Normalized data model (user, from the todo list):** the stored model should look like `project { edges: edge[]; ... }`, `edge { start: node; end: node; curve_radius: float }`, `node { position: vec2 }`. **Comparison with the current model (Claude, after ^105):** today a road is `Path { nodes: {x, y, th}[]; radii: (number | null)[]; shapes: (ShapeTag | null)[] }`, edge i joins nodes i and i+1, and shared nodes are nodes of different roads at the same position (^92). The sketch is a graph, which is the right direction for junctions (^23) and for ^100, but it is not complete yet: (1) it has no **heading** per node; two positions and a radius do not fix a curve (an arc needs the heading at an end, an S-curve or hook needs both), so the heading stays (one per node, which fits shared nodes); (2) `curve_radius` must be allowed to be **auto** (null: the largest radius that fits, ^8), a plain float would freeze it; (3) the **shape tag** (bend, S with its sign and optional fixed middle line, hook with its sign, ^7) decides between curves that two headings and a radius allow, and keeps it stable; (4) the **lane width** (3 m, `LANE_WIDTH_M`) will be per edge (^26); (5) nodes need **ids** so that edges reference them, which makes the sharing explicit instead of by position; (6) **roads** (chains) are used by the editor (the road being drawn and its end, extending, select lane ^76, the direction arrows) and would become derived as maximal chains through nodes with one edge in and one out, or stay as a grouping. A target for discussion: `node { id, position, heading }`, `edge { id, start, end, radius: number | null, shape: ShapeTag | null }` in `project { nodes, edges }`. It changes the selection (`NodeRef` is road and index today), history, hit-testing and rendering, so it comes before persistence (^82) so that no migrations are needed. |

The text has a right border (a white line to its right). The panel can hold several items at the same time, and the interval is counted for each item individually. Redos appear there too, with the prefix `redo: `. Builds on the steps of ^88. Naming (^111): this is the *recent history panel*. |

The panel itself (the steps) is not hidden by this. Assumed (Claude): the interval is 2 minutes of not using a control; the hint comes back when it runs out. Acceptance test (user): on a blank canvas in select mode `[LMB]: draw mode` is the only control hint when the camera controls and the select controls are hidden. **Revised (user, milestone ^151):** the interval is 5 minutes for now, not 2. The term for such a control is *visited control* and for one that is never hidden *unvisitable* (user; README glossary; first called *learned control*, which the user found confusing; in the code still `learned`, `LEARNED_MS`, renamed in milestone ^153). |

| ^130 | decision | decided | **Terms arm, road, branch (user):** *arm* is any set of connected nodes; today the same as what was called road, since segments cannot run parallel yet. *Road* will mean all arms connected by at least one parallel segment, e.g. an "H" whose top and bottom half only touch but share no nodes. *Branch* means both the concept of splits and merges and a chain of nodes without any: its first and last node have a number of edges other than two, all nodes between have exactly two. A "T" has three branches (the stem and the two wings), an "L" is one branch with bends, an "A" has four (roof, bar, left leg, right leg). | ^29 | decision | decided (principles) | **UI design principles:** minimal chrome, canvas (almost) full-screen; progressive disclosure (show tools, options and handles only when relevant, e.g. only for the selection or active tool); Fitts's law (frequent targets big and near the pointer, edges and corners are cheap targets); Hick's law (few choices at a time, group and hide the rest; the user wrote "Higgs law", read as Hick's); consistency with the handle vocabulary (^9) and keymap (^11); discoverability through hover hints rather than permanent legends. The current toolbar and legend of ^13 are provisional and will be replaced in ^20. |
| ^30 | decision | decided | **Camera and pointer controls (supersedes ^14, answers ^28).** Mouse: LMB drag = selection box (later; nothing yet on empty space); RMB drag = pan; MMB drag = rotate and precise zoom about the point where the drag started: horizontal movement rotates (right = clockwise), vertical movement zooms (up = zoom in), like Blender's Ctrl+MMB (revised by the user after testing; originally vertical movement changed the rotation sensitivity). Wheel = zoom at the cursor. While rotating: Esc cancels the rotation (restores the view from before the drag); N, E, S, W set the rotation so that north/east/south/west points up (N = reset); the cursor turns into a compass (needle points to world north) with N, E, S, W key chips, which can also be clicked (LMB while MMB is held); layout in ^33. Touch: two-finger twist rotates (about the finger midpoint), the same gesture also pinch-zooms and pans; one finger drags handles, or pans on empty space. The grid is pinned to the camera: it stays axis-aligned to the screen and does not move at all while rotating (it has its own center in the view, separate from the camera center, which moves when rotating about an off-center pivot); it only follows panning and zooming (revised after testing). |
| ^31 | decision | assumed | **Details I filled in for ^30:** (a) rotation is 0.4 degrees per horizontal pixel; (b) zoom is exponential, 1% per vertical pixel from the start of the drag (scale = scale at start x e^(0.01 x pixels moved up)), so returning to the starting height returns to the starting zoom; (f) a per-axis dead zone of 20 pixels around the drag start (8 at first, raised by the user after testing): horizontal movement within it does not rotate and vertical movement within it does not zoom, so a mostly vertical drag does not also rotate a little and vice versa; outside it the effect starts from zero (no jump), and after an N/E/S/W jump the dead zone is re-centered on the pointer. This recreates Blender's "Dolly" zoom style, where the zoom factor is 1 + (distance moved) x 0.01 per pixel, dragging up zooms in; the exact current Blender source could not be read (projects.blender.org is not reachable from this session), the exponential form matches it to first order and never degenerates; (c) a right click without movement still removes a node (kept from ^11), a right drag never does; (d) the compass only exists during the middle-button drag, there is no permanent rotation indicator yet; (e) the world rotation is stored in the view as `rot` radians, positive = clockwise on screen. |
| ^32 | decision | decided | **UI principles (user):** (1) always good contrast and readability (WCAG: 4.5:1 for text, 3:1 for graphics and component edges, in light and dark mode; guarded by `src/lib/contrast.test.ts`, so new color tokens must be added to its pair list); (2) think in components: reuse symbols, colors, styles and icons. The **KeyCap** component (`KeyCap.svelte`: raised cap with a thick bottom edge, turns blue and sinks in while the key is pressed) is the one look for every hotkey hint (toolbar, legend, compass) and for key-like drag hints. Icons live in `Icon.svelte`. Colors are CSS tokens in `app.css`, including text variants (`red-ink`, `orange-ink`) for labels. Extends ^29. |

| ^46 | bug | fixed | When the cursor was at 90 degrees or more from the heading arrow (e.g. behind it), the new segment became a straight line, until the node was moved. Cause: the new node's heading mirrors the arrow about the chord, so one circle passes through both nodes; as a hook (two arcs of one radius) that circle has coinciding arc centers, the straight middle piece has no direction, and the hook construction gave up, falling back to a line. Fix: coinciding centers are treated as one big circular arc split in two halves, and if a turn sense fails the other is tried before falling back to a line. Previews and created curves use the same code, so both are right. Reported by the user. |

| ^92 | decision | superseded by the graph (^106, ^170): nodes are shared by id now | **Shared nodes by position (^23):** the document stays a list of roads (chains). Roads meet at shared nodes, and a shared node is simply nodes of different roads *at the same position* (within 1 µm): no ids or links are stored, so undo, insert and remove need no bookkeeping, and every road keeps its own copy of the node with its heading. Consequences: the selection is always closed over shared nodes (select, move and delete act on all copies); a heading change is copied to the other copies (and releases the fixed middle lines of their S-curves); the junction of a split has one edge in and two out, a merge two in and one out (`junctions()` in `src/lib/curves/junctions.ts`, not used by the UI yet). Trade-off: two roads whose nodes happen to be at exactly the same position are joined; undoing or deleting works per node, so a shared node that is deleted disappears from all roads (a road left with one node disappears). Chosen over a graph model (node ids and edge lists) because it keeps all curve code and the history as they are; if lanes get connection rules later (turns, priorities) a stored graph may be needed. |

The old behaviour of ^65 to switch to another road's end while drawing (dropping a one-node road) is gone, because that click now merges. Extending backwards makes a merge a split (the clicked node is where the road starts). Esc after a split on a segment keeps the inserted node. |

| ^172 | decision | decided (user); the math pointers: assumed | **No shape tag (user, experiment `experiment/no-shape-tag`, PR #19, after the discussion of ^161):** an edge is stored as `id, start, end, radius, laneConfigurationId`; only the radius can be null (automatic). | ^173 | decision | decided (user: agreed); built in the persistence PR: version 1, the migration step and the checks (^177) | **Migrations of the stored model (user, ^148):** `version` of a project is an integer. Loading a project with an older version runs the migrations in order (`1 → 2`, `2 → 3`, ...); each is a pure function on the parsed JSON, and old migrations are never edited. Each version keeps a sample project file in the tests; a test migrates every sample to the current version and checks the result. A project with a newer version than the app knows is refused with a clear message, never opened half. Every change to the stored model (`docs/data-model.md`) needs both of us to agree, a version bump and such a migration. |
| ^174 | decision | assumed | **Details of the model swap (milestone ^149, Claude):** (1) the editor holds a `Graph` (`model/graph.ts`: nodes with id and heading, edges with id, start, end, radius and lane, lane configurations with a width in meters); the file format with millimeters is the persistence PR. (2) *Chains* (`editor/chains.ts`) are derived from the graph for the curve code, drawing and hit testing: runs of edges through nodes with exactly one edge in and one out; a node without an edge is a chain of its own; every handle carries the id of its node or edge. (3) The road being drawn is the node drawn from (`active`), which way the next edge runs (`end`) and the node it started at (`origin`); extending an existing road starts at its far end, a split at the split node. (4) A click while drawing: the node being drawn from finishes, `origin` closes a loop, any other node merges into it (before, a click on a middle node of the road's own chain did nothing). (5) Deleting a node with one edge in and one out joins its neighbours; deleting any other node removes its edges; nodes left without an edge go, except the one being drawn from. (6) The corner points of a hook are derived and drawn as hollow rings (^105); those of an S-curve stay draggable and turn the heading (^172). (7) The road surface takes its width from the lane configuration of the first edge of a chain. (8) Tests convert the demo paths to graphs with a test-only helper (`testing/graphs.ts`). Not in this PR: persistence (^82, ^163 to ^168, ^171) and the dropped switch of the drawing end has no replacement yet. |

The dev-only test hook is not in the build. No routing, so no 404 fallback is needed. |

| ^177 | decision | assumed | **Details of persistence (milestone ^149, Claude):** (1) a project is kept in IndexedDB (`modelcity`, store `projects`), one record `{ id, file }` per project, the file being exactly an export file; the id of the record is not part of the file. (2) Autosave 400 ms after the graph changed (`ProjectSession.changed`), at the latest 5 s after the oldest unsaved change (^183), also on `pagehide`; the open project is saved before another one is opened or created. (3) The last open project is remembered in `localStorage` (`modelcity.project`), else the newest opens, else there is no project (^189). (4) The editor rounds lengths to the millimeter when it commits a step (`roundToMm`), so a saved project equals what was shown. (5) Imported files become new projects (the file has no id); a file that is no project is refused with a message and the open project stays. (6) Export file name: the title with other characters as `-`, plus `.modelcity.json`. (7) Every new project is called "Untitled project" until renamed (pen icon on its card in the projects modal, `Ctrl+O`: Enter or the check mark renames, Esc or the cross cancels without closing; a rename is written at once, the modal is blocked while it is written (`busy`), and the date of the last change stays (a title is metadata like the camera; a file manager does not change the date of a file when it is renamed either, and the list does not jump while projects are renamed); a title is at most 100 characters). Delete asks first, in the card itself (check mark on the left to confirm, cross on the right to cancel) and deletes for good: no bin, no `deletedAt`; export is the backup. The modal has a search field (fuzzy, ^178), `New project`, `Import file…`, a cross in the top right corner (no extra close button), and Esc closes it; while it is up the control hints panel shows its own controls only (^168). A click on the open project's card closes the modal; the open project's card has the italic label "open" on its own line (user: clearer than "current project") (not only the outline). The check mark to confirm a delete stays a check mark, always red (Claude's view: check mark and cross are the same confirm and cancel pair as in the rename, in the same two places). Opening or leaving a project never changes a date: only a change of the map does (the camera is saved on leaving without touching the date). All cards of the modal are full width with their icons inside; below the list are the cards `Import file…`, `Save to disc…` and `New project`. (8) `Ctrl+S` and `Ctrl+O` override the browser's save and open page. (9) Migrations: `MIGRATIONS` is empty at version 1; the sample files of every version live in `src/lib/project/samples/` and a test brings each to the current version. |
| ^178 | decision | decided (user; the feature itself: ^427) | **Every list is searchable (user, a general design principle):** all lists the user sees (projects, later the operations search ^97, the history, ...) have a search field, and the search is fuzzy: the characters of the query in order, ranked by how well they match (`src/lib/ui/fuzzy.ts`: characters that follow each other and at word starts score more, equal matches keep their order, so the newest stay first). First applied to the projects modal. Noted in `CLAUDE.md`. |

| ^182 | feature | idea (no milestone yet) | **Showing waiting consistently (user's question):** loading and saving are fast today (IndexedDB, a few milliseconds), so a rename only blocks the modal (`busy`, no visible screen). When something takes longer (a big import, Supabase ^176, previews ^179) the app needs one way to say so: an agreed look (a blocking overlay for what must finish first, a quiet indicator for background work such as autosave), when it appears (only after a short delay, so quick things do not flash), what it says, and how it can fail (an error with a way to retry). Claude's suggestion: decide it when the first slow operation exists, as a small shared piece (`busy` state plus one component), not before. |
| ^183 | decision | open (the numbers: assumed); **maintained in ^445, ^446, ^447 (the numbers; the open questions stay here)** (this hat stays as history) | **Autosave: when and how (user: discuss and document):** built so far (assumed): a debounce (a change restarts a 400 ms timer) with a maximum wait of 5 s (a throttle: while changes keep coming, the oldest unsaved one waits at most 5 s), plus a save when the page is hidden (`pagehide`; the write is asynchronous and can be lost if the tab dies at once). Open: (1) how long a save takes in the worst case (turning the graph into the file, then the IndexedDB write): measure with big maps (hundreds of roads, with the performance tests ^122); the maximum wait must be clearly longer than that, so that saves do not pile up, and the debounce delay clearly shorter than the maximum wait. (2) What happens if a save fails (storage full, private mode): say so to the user, keep the map in memory, retry; there is no message yet. (3) Whether saves may overlap (a second one starts while the first writes; IndexedDB keeps their order). (4) Whether a save on closing the page needs `beforeunload` too. Claude's recommendation: keep the numbers until measured, then decide (1) and (2) together with ^182 (showing waiting) and ^185. |
| ^184 | feature | idea (no milestone yet) | **Saving in the background (user's idea):** do the work of a save (turning the graph into the file and writing it to IndexedDB) in a Web Worker, so that a big map never stops the editor. Claude's notes: IndexedDB works in workers; the graph still has to be copied to the worker (a structured clone on the main thread), which is the part that would remain; a packed form of the nodes and edges (typed arrays, transferable) would make that copy cheap; worth it only if ^183 measures that saving is slow. |
| ^185 | feature | done (milestone ^151, PR #28); **maintained in ^440, ^441, ^442** (this hat stays as history) | **A notification when saved (user's idea):** show a toast or not. Claude's take: no toast for autosave (it would appear every few seconds and say nothing new); at most a quiet "saved" mark that is only there while there is something to say; a toast (or a message in the modal) for what the user asked for (export done, import done) and always for a failed save (^183). To be decided together with ^182. **Decided (user, milestone ^151):** toasts (^232) confirm what the user asked for: `Ctrl+S` says `saved`; confirmations in general use the same toasts. Autosave stays quiet. A failed save is ^183. |
| ^186 | decision | decided (user: no design for phones and touch now; viewing on phones comes later as ^104); open: N x M, the notice page | **Target devices, screen sizes and input (user's question):** user's opinion: do not design for phones and touch devices now; show a plain page saying "not available on touch devices", and when the screen is too small, "screen too small, at least N x M". Laptops with a trackpad are considered, but not designed for. Claude's take: (1) agree, desktop first. (2) Detect "no mouse or trackpad at all" with `matchMedia('(any-pointer: fine)')` (false on a phone or a tablet without accessories); not `(pointer: coarse)` or the user agent, which would also shut out touch laptops and tablets with a mouse. (3) The size check must follow the window (resize), not run once; suggested minimum 900 x 600 CSS pixels (the hints panel needs about 360 px of height in select mode, the projects modal about 450 px of width; every laptop screen is larger, so only small windows see it, and the page shows the current size so that the user can resize). (4) The touch code exists (one finger as the left button, two fingers for the camera, ^30, ^40, ^104): switching it off makes it a hidden feature, so keep it behind a flag in `src/lib/features.ts` with this hat as the reason and "touch support is wanted" as what decides its return, rather than deleting it. (5) What a trackpad lacks: the middle button (it rotates, ^30; the keys of the compass do the same), and the right button is a two finger click (dragging with it is awkward, it pans); also the wheel zooms, so a two finger scroll zooms. (6) A browser cannot say how many buttons a mouse has, and a trackpad reports itself as a mouse (`pointerType` is `mouse`); the wheel events of a trackpad can be told apart only by guessing (small fractional deltas, pinch as a wheel with `ctrlKey`), which is unreliable, so no detection: offer keyboard alternatives instead (^187). Open: N x M, and whether the notice page gets built now. **Decided (user):** phones and touch devices are not designed for now; the time goes to features. Eventually a phone should at least be able to *view* a map: that is the view mode ^104, to come after GitHub Pages (^175) and Supabase (^176), because before those a map cannot be made on a phone or brought there without export and import; it needs a design of its own. Claude's addition (assumed): until then the notice page (touch and too small) is not built either; the app just stays as it is on such devices. |

Claude's recommendation: not before ^186 says laptops are a target. |

A batch with more than 5 steps shows its last 4 and `(N more steps)`. **Revised again (user):** selection steps are one row in the panel (`select 3 nodes`, named by what the run selected in the end; a run that ends where it began, such as select then deselect all, changed nothing and has no row, but its steps stay in the history); no batch titles; every step has its own line on the right, joined with its neighbours' in a batch, and an undone step grays its line out as well. |

| ^224 | feature | idea (no milestone yet; user: an experiment for later, a fundamental change) | **Independent ticks, curves that are not circular (user's idea):** today the two ticks of a bend, the points where the straight pieces touch the arc (the radius points at the ends, ^128), are interlocked: they sit at equal distances from the corner, symmetric around the bisector, and so the curve is a circular arc (`R`, ^6, ^8). The idea: let them move independently. The curve between them would then not be a circular arc but, for example, an elliptical one (a conic section). **Claude's comment:** (1) what it buys: asymmetric corners (a sharp entry and a long exit, like a ramp or the easement curves of real roads), more control with the same two lines. (2) What it costs: it replaces the curve family, since all the curve code, the hit testing (`nearestParam` and `distanceToPiece` are exact for lines and arcs), the arrows, the radius labels and the handles (^128) assume arcs and lines; "the radius" no longer means anything for such a curve (two lengths instead, or a conic weight); the stored model changes (`radius` of an edge becomes two values; fixed by ^170, so it needs the user's agreement, a version bump and a migration, ^173); and offset curves, which lanes (^22, ^26, ^156), markings and junctions (^24) need, are exact for arcs and lines but not for ellipses (the parallel curve of an ellipse is no ellipse, so lanes would be approximated). (3) A cheaper way to try the same effect: two circular arcs with different radii that join smoothly (a biarc, which `biarc.ts` and the S-curves already use), so the geometry stays lines and arcs and offsets stay exact; the second tick would then set the second radius. Suggestion: try that first, as an experiment branch like ^172, before touching the stored model. Not decided. |

| ^227 | bug | fixed (branch `bugfix/empty-history-steps`) | **Empty steps in the history (user):** finishing a road without ever placing a node (`Esc` right after entering draw mode) made a step `finish road` that changed nothing. Cause: a step was recorded when the graph was a *different object*, and `removeNodes` returned a new graph every time, even with nothing to remove. **All steps checked (Claude):** (1) finish road with no node: empty, fixed (`removeNodes` returns the same graph when nothing goes). (2) A drag that ends where it began (drag away and back, with a node, a heading, a radius or a group): the graph was a new object with the same content, so `move node` was recorded; fixed: `sameGraph` compares the content, and an equal graph stays the object it was (so autosave, which compares objects, does not save for nothing either). It is then a selection step if the press changed the selection. (3) A selection box or a click that selects the same nodes in another order: the order counted as a change (and the name was `deselect 0 nodes`); fixed: the selection is compared as a set. (4) Not empty, left as they are: finishing a road that has nodes (it leaves draw mode and clears the selection); placing, inserting, splitting, merging, extending (each changes the graph or the drawing state); deleting (always removes something); a click on a node that changes the selection. Splitting or extending a node that is selected already changes only the drawing state, which is no step (^88: a step changes the roads or the selection), as before. Tests in `steps.test.ts` and `graph.test.ts`. |
| ^228 | bug | merging done (milestone ^153, ^248); the repairs on import are later (^229) | **Two nodes can occupy the same position (user's question):** the stored lengths are integer millimeters (^170) so that there are no "equal, but not equal" positions; nothing in the app stops two nodes from being at the same millimeter. **Checked (Claude):** (1) *dragging a node onto another node*: both stay, at the same position (tried: nodes 0 and 2 both at 0,0), with no merge and no warning. The same for a group move that ends on a node, and after rounding to the millimeter two nodes that were a little apart can become equal. (2) *Clicking* cannot do it: a click within 9 px of a node hits that node (a split, merge or finish), a click on a curve closer than 0.5 m to a node does not insert (^137). (3) *Import* accepts it: the checks of a file (`^192` to `^208`) are about ids and references, not positions. (4) What it does to the model: an edge between two nodes at the same position has no length (the curve code treats it as a degenerate piece), and the chains, junction and branch code cannot tell the two nodes apart on the map. **Options (Claude):** (A) forbid it: a dragged node cannot be dropped on another node's position (it stays at the last free one); the smallest rule is exactly the same millimeter, a larger one a minimum gap (0.5 m, `RMIN`, below which curves cannot be bent, ^57); (B) merge: dropping a node on another joins the two (a topology change, which belongs to the snap points of ^101 and milestone ^153); (C) allow it and let the validator (^169) report it. **Recommendation:** (A) now with the minimum gap decided by the user (my suggestion: exactly equal millimeters are refused, and the 0.5 m gap goes with ^137), (B) later with the snapping engine, and a refusal reason for files that have it, as a new import hat (rule ^209) if the user wants files refused (assumed: not; ^169 reports it instead). Not decided. **Decided (user, milestone ^153): option B, merging, is in the snapping milestone: ^248.** **Decided (user, milestone ^151):** no temporary rule against dropping a node on another node: it depends on the snapping (^101, ^153) and is not worth a rule of its own; the repairs (below) are not in ^151 either. **Added (user): repairs on import.** A file with such nodes (or any other problem that can be fixed without asking) is not refused but repaired, silently as far as the map goes, and the user is told once: a *warning* notice (orange triangle, ^210) such as "This file had issues but was repaired. Details are in the browser console (F12)." The console gets one line per repair, `[modelcity] import repaired (^N): detail`, like the refusals (^209). Notes (Claude): (1) every repair is a decision about the user's data, so every repair gets its own hat that says what it changes and why, like the refusals (^192 to ^208): rule ^209 and `.claude/CLAUDE.md` are extended to repairs when this is built; (2) a repair must be deterministic and must not hide data loss: assumed for equal positions: the later node is moved by 1 mm steps along x until its position is free (keeps the topology and both nodes visible; merging them would change the topology and loses a node), to be decided; (3) the modal closes after a successful import today; with a repair it stays open and shows the warning above the list, the project is imported already (assumed); (4) refused stays refused: a file that cannot be understood (^192 to ^208) is not repaired; (5) the validator (^169) and the repairs share their checks: one list of checks, each marked refuse, repair or report; (6) the same repair also applies to what is loaded from the browser storage and later from Supabase, and a repair there is saved at once: ^229. |
| ^229 | feature | done in part (milestone ^151, PR #28: the pipeline without repairs, the toast, the version label); the repairs and saving a migrated project back are later | **One load pipeline: repairs apply to every source, and a repair is saved at once (user):** what ^228 says about repairing a file on import (a warning notice, details in the console, one hat per repair) applies just as much to a project that is loaded from the browser storage (IndexedDB), and later from Supabase (^176): the same violations (two nodes at one position, ...) are repaired, the user is warned, and the repaired project is saved right after (the same record, in place), so the repair happens once. **Finding (Claude):** today only an *import* goes through `parseProject` (migration, then the checks, ^173, ^192 to ^208); a project loaded from the browser storage goes straight into `fromFile` (`ProjectSession.show`), with no migration and no checks. Nothing is wrong yet (the version is 1 and there are no migrations), but the first version bump would leave the saved projects of the browser unmigrated; this has to be one pipeline anyway: *read the record or file, migrate, check, repair, convert* (in `project/`), used by import, browser storage and Supabase. **Notes (Claude), agreed in principle:** (1) the repair save does not touch the dates (it is no change of the map by the user, like opening or renaming, ^177, ^214: `save(false)`); (2) the warning is shown once per repair, not at every load: after the save the stored project is clean; (3) the original is not lost: the console line of each repair says what it was before (positions, ids), and a repair is only ever one of a short list of deterministic ones (^228); (4) for Supabase: write the repair back only if the row has not changed meanwhile (the conflict rules of ^176 decide how); a project of a newer version than the app is never repaired or saved, only refused or shown read-only (^195); (5) for the stored record of the browser the pipeline runs for every project in the list when it is shown, or only for the one that is opened (assumed: only when opened, so the list stays fast; a broken card still shows its title). **Narrowed again (user, milestone ^151):** the pipeline is built now, *without repairs* and without saving back: the same converters as an import migrate a project from the browser storage when it is opened, and if there is no converter for its version that is an error (the refusal `old`, ^196). The browser storage can hold old records (a record stays as it was written until the project is saved again, so after a version bump the old ones stay old). A card of a project of an older version shows the label `version N` after its title (the label always fits: a long title is cut off earlier, ^188). Saving a migrated project back at once comes later, together with the repairs. **Before that:** the pipeline is built now, *without repairs*: what is loaded from the browser storage goes through the same migration and checks as an import. If the project that was open last cannot be read, the app does not stop: it shows the same refusal as an import would (same kinds and texts, ^192 to ^209) as a toast (^232), keeps the stored project as it is, and starts with a new, empty project. Opening such a card in the modal shows the same toast. (Saving back at once: later, see above.) A project whose file is too damaged to have a title shows a placeholder title on its card. The repairs, the repair warning and their hats (^228) stay for a later milestone. Links: ^228 (the repairs), ^173 (migrations), ^176 (Supabase), ^169 (validator), ^209 (rule). |

| ^236 | bug | fixed (milestone ^151, PR #28; user's report: the camera feels bugged when opening another project) | **The camera of a project was not restored correctly (user):** two causes, both found with tests. (1) When a project is opened its camera was put back while the camera still kept the view on the content of the project before, which pulled the stored view toward that content (tried: a stored x of 100 came back as 203.8). (2) While the canvas has not been measured yet (the size is 1 x 1) the view was fitted to that size, which moved it at start. Fix: the camera learns the content of the new project before its stored view is put back, and a view is not fitted to a canvas that is not measured. Tests: `project/cameraSaved.test.ts` and an e2e scenario (two projects, each with its own camera, switched and reloaded). (3) Moving the camera alone was never saved (by the old rule of ^166 and ^177), and the save when the page closes is asynchronous and can be lost, so after a reload the camera of the open project could be back where it was at the last change. Fix: the camera is saved once it has rested for 1 second (`CAMERA_SAVE_MS`), without changing the date, and only if it is not where it was saved or loaded. |

Same cause, and the same again for the hovered segment and branch: the outline only knew about Alt from the last mouse move, and a pan, zoom or rotate changes what is under a still pointer. **General fix (user: "this bug occurs often"):** what is under the pointer is no longer a value that every operation has to remember to refresh; it is *derived* (`hover` in `editor.svelte.ts`) from the last pointer position (with its Alt and Shift) and the state of the editor, so it is right after anything that changes either. Pressing or releasing Alt or Shift reaches the editor through the key events (`keydown` and `keyup` fire for Alt itself, with no mouse move; a window that loses the focus resets them), and a change of the view (`camera.view`) re-feeds the same screen position (`MapCanvas`, `Editor.look`). Tests: `editor/controls.test.ts`, an e2e scenario, and a general one, `editor/hoverFresh.test.ts`: random sequences of operations (keys, undo, clicks, right clicks, boxes, drags, Alt, draw mode) and after each one what is under the pointer (hover, hint target, cursor, focus) must be what a fresh look at the same place gives. Fix: a new state of the road being drawn, `first` (`DrawEnd` in `model/junction.ts`): right after that click, a node placed next still goes before it, but a merge runs out of the node, in the order of the clicks (not when it closes the loop at the node where the road started). Clicking D first and then A gives D to A. The merge preview follows the same rule. Tests in `editor/extend.test.ts`. The workaround (extend further back, connect, delete) is no longer needed. |

| ^245 | question | open (no milestone yet; user: not sure whether it will or should be done, recorded so that the question is documented) | **True segment selection instead of node selection (user's question):** today a selection is a list of *node* ids (`Editor.selection`, ^70) and a *selected segment* is derived from it: an edge whose two nodes are both selected (`selectedSegments`, ^133, ^231). The question is the other way around: the selection holds *segments* (edge ids) and the selected nodes are derived (the ends of the selected segments, plus nodes that were selected alone). **What the node-based selection cannot say:** (1) segments A-B and C-D without B-C when B-C exists (all four nodes selected means B-C is selected too); (2) one of two edges between the same two nodes (the model allows it, drawing does not today, ^100); (3) a whole multi-lane road, or one lane of it (^22, ^130), which are neither nodes nor single segments. **What true segment selection would change:** a selection of edge ids and of node ids that no selected segment reaches (a road's first node has no segment); the selection box and clicks (a click on a curve selects that segment, a box selects segments by what rule: fully inside, or crossing?); moving, deleting and the rings of ^114 to ^116 act on the ends of the selected segments; copying and pasting (^117) get a natural unit (segments with their nodes); the names of history steps (`select 3 nodes` would become `select 3 segments`, ^216); the control hints and the glossary (*selected segment*); the file format is not affected (the selection is not stored). **Claude's view (assumed):** node selection is enough for the common case (selecting the two nodes of a segment selects exactly that segment); decide when lanes (^156) or the selection transforms and the clipboard (^154) are planned, since those are what could make segments the natural unit; not before. **Open:** whether to do it at all; whether a mixed selection (segments and lone nodes) is wanted or segments only plus lone nodes as a special case; what a box selects. |

| ^247 | question | decided (user: close it); done in PR #45: one constant `SAME_PLACE_M` in `curves/tolerance.ts`, `SAME_WAY` for headings | **Millimeter precision against exact geometry (user's question, milestone ^153):** positions are integer millimeters (^170) but headings are continuous, so a position that lies exactly on a snap line or on the touching point of two circles (^100, ^101) is an irrational number for any heading that is not a multiple of 90°; rounding to the millimeter moves it by up to 0.7 mm, so the "exactly" in ^100 (distance exactly one lane width, circles touching at exactly one point, concentric radii) cannot hold bit for bit. **Options:** (1) keep millimeters and compare with a tolerance (assumed: 1 mm for distances, a tiny angle for headings, which are stored as floats and can be exactly equal or opposite); (2) micrometers (1e-6): the error shrinks a thousand times but never vanishes, the file grows, it is a data model change with a migration (^173), so it does not solve the problem, only moves it; (3) stored constraints between nodes: a model change, much bigger. **Where 0.7 mm comes from (Claude):** node B placed on a circle around node A, `d = (w(A) + w(B)) / 2` (3000 mm now, an integer; half-integer for other widths), at an angle that makes the exact point irrational. The integer position is the exact point rounded in each coordinate, so it is off by at most (0.5, 0.5), a length of at most `sqrt(0.5² + 0.5²) = 0.707 mm`; by the triangle inequality the distance to A is off `d` by at most that, in the worst direction (diagonals between the millimeter lattice points), and 0 along the axes. **Update (user):** floats for positions as an alternative (a data model change), merging nodes by a distance threshold shown as a gizmo circle; or millimeters with a merge radius large enough to tell touching from not touching. **Claude's view:** floats do not help: irrational numbers do not exist in floats either, and the error (about 1e-12 m) is only smaller, so a tolerance is needed anyway; JSON also round-trips floats exactly, so the model would change for nothing. With millimeters two numbers must not be confused: the *tolerance* of the decisions (1 mm: bigger than the rounding error of 0.71 mm, and every relation is checked pairwise between a node and its neighbour, so the errors do not add up) and the *merge radius* of the snap (in pixels, a visible ring around the snap point, much bigger). A deliberate placement is never within 1 mm of touching; a node that is by chance is treated as touching, which is harmless. **Recommendation: (1).** Exactness is only needed for decisions (are these two segments parallel, do these circles touch), never for what is drawn, and every CAD decides such things with a tolerance; the snapped position is computed in floats and rounded once. Parallel and touching are derived from headings, positions and lane widths whenever they are needed, never stored (the rule on derived state in `CLAUDE.md`). **Closed (^816):** where "exact" is used today: `towers.ts`, `parallel.ts`, `sharedRadius.ts` each declare their own `TOL_M = 1e-3` (1 mm: nodes one lane width apart and on a shared normal, concentric centers, the radius that equals the largest); headings of towers are equal within `1e-9` of the dot product (about 0.003°); the radius point stays on its line within 5 cm (`LOCUS_TOL_M`, `snapping.ts`); nodes merge within 0.1 m (`MERGE_WORLD_M`, `drop.ts`, `snapping.ts`: meters in the world, not pixels); snap suggestions are offered within 25 px and 300 px (`engine.ts`, screen pixels); crossings use `1e-6` (`geometry`). The 1 mm tolerates the 0.71 mm of rounding to whole millimeters. One decision declared three times: milestone ^816 makes it one constant. |

Assumed details: the dragged node goes, the one dropped on stays with its position and heading; **all merges are accepted and the node dropped on keeps its heading (user, changed his mind: no refusal, no toast, no refusal ring); the edges of the dragged node then take that tangent, so their curves may change shape (a head-on meeting may become a hook; the validator may report it, ^169);** the edges of the dragged node are attached to the stayed node; an edge between the two (it would have no length) is removed; the edges that would run twice between the same two nodes in the same direction stay (a duplicate edge is not our problem here, ^245); one history step (undo gives both nodes back, ^81); a group move that ends on a node merges the dragged node of the group the same way; after rounding to the millimeter equal positions are merged too. | ^299 | feature | maintained in ^1064 (user: the automatic collapse is dropped, a control collapses the selected nodes instead); was: planned (milestone ^829; user: later, not yet; ^365 repeats it, with open questions: which properties must be equal (lane width, later slope and height ^304), automatic or only offered, pinned or selected nodes, undo; there are more considerations and caveats, the hat is a placeholder) | **Nodes without an effect collapse (user):** a node must have an effect, or it is removed: for example a node inserted into a curve that changes nothing about its shape (same heading and radius at both sides, a through node with a straight continuation, ^10) is merged away. Not yet: today such nodes stay (^276, ^298). **Caveats (user):** it is more complicated than this; especially nodes of *parallel* segments (^100): a node that has no effect for its own segment may be needed so that the segments next to it stay parallel and concentric (^281, ^282), so it must not collapse; the other way round the user wants such parallel nodes to be *forced into existence* where they do not exist: when a segment runs parallel to another, the nodes that make it parallel (one lane width away, same heading) are created. What counts as an effect (a junction, a parallel node, a changed radius, a marking, ^21) is to be designed together with lanes (^156) and the validator (^169). **Update (user, ^814):** "parallel nodes are forced into existence" is done by derived nodes (^822). Proposed definition of an effect: a node must justify its existence by (a) it has more (split, merge) or fewer (leaf) than two segments, or (b) it is one end of a bend or S-curve/hook. **Claude:** (a) yes. (b) is too wide: two bends on the same circle meet at a node that is the end of both and still has no effect (removing it gives the same result, like ^814). The rule is: a node with two segments has no effect if one segment from its neighbour to its neighbour looks the same (straight on; the same circle with the same radius and turn; the S-curve of ^823 with equal arcs), **and** the two segments are equal in everything else (lane configuration, later slope, height and markings). A change of radius, of turn or of lane width is an effect. Also: it runs after each operation inside the same history step (one undo), never on loading a project, never on selected nodes or nodes being drawn or dragged, and a derived node that is realized (^824) and dropped without a move dissolves at once (that is consistent: it has no effect). **Open:** pinned nodes; "the same result" needs the tolerance of ^247. **Restated (user asked):** a node B between the segments A-B and B-C (A and C are the nodes at the far ends of the two segments) has no effect if the one segment A-C would draw exactly the same curve through the place of B with the same heading, and the two segments agree in everything else (lane configuration, later slope, height, markings). |

Existing hats are reused where they already describe exactly that one thing; a hat that describes several (for example ^54: enter draw mode, leave it, the first node) stays as the decision, and each one gets its own hat that points to it. |

| ^435 | decision | decided (user: one name shared by the hint and the history); assumed (details: the internal name is separate, several operations may share a name, steps may add detail) | **One user-facing name per operation (user, audit ^371):** the control hint and the history step of an operation use the same name, no aliases (`place node`). The operation hat has its own unique internal name (`place first node`, ^409); several operations may share one user-facing name when context tells them apart; a history step may add detail after it (`select 3 nodes`, `move 3 nodes`). Known mismatches, not fixed in the audit: `add node` vs `place node` (^421), `finish` (hint, ^142) vs `finish road` (history step); to be decided by the user which side changes. |

| ^437 | operation | done (found in the audit ^371; `finish road` in the code, ^216, ^239) | **Cancel first node (operation, user):** with only the first node placed (no segment yet), a click on it or `Esc` takes it away again and goes back to draw mode; nothing is placed. It is a step of its own in the history, so that undo brings the node back at the exact place. The control hint should say `cancel` (today `finish`); the history step is today called `finish road`, and the node is removed because a lone node is no road (^322). Control: ^436. Origin: ^239, ^120. |
| ^438 | bug | done (milestone ^791, PR #35; user) | **Cancelling the first node says `finish` and `finish road` (user):** the hint over the start node of a road with one node reads `LMB or Esc: finish` and the history step reads `finish road`; both should say `cancel` (operation `cancel first node`, ^437), because nothing is finished and the node is removed. By ^181 a hint says what the input does now. Follows ^435 (one name for the hint and the step). |
| ^439 | decision | assumed (user's model, Claude agrees; details assumed) | **Operations and batches (user, audit ^371):** an *operation* is atomic: one control use gives one history step, however many nodes it touches. A *batch* is not an operation; it is a run of consecutive steps that undo and redo together: the steps of one drawing session (from the first node until the road is finished or cancelled), or a run of selection steps (^103, ^108, ^216; `kind` and `continues` in `history.ts`). A batch has no hat and no control; its title comes from its steps. The sub-steps of a batch are operations with hats of their own (`place first node` then `cancel first node` is a batch of two). A step that only undoes another (cancel) is still recorded, so that undo can bring the node back. |

| ^483 | decision | decided (user; done in milestone ^794, PR #36) | **The step of the extend lines (user): 7.5° from the heading, 22 lines:** the extend lines (^325 to ^330, ^337 to ^340) are at 15° to 75° in steps of 15°, on both sides, today (`extendRules` in `snap/rules.ts`); the user suggested 15° instead of 30° and asked about 5°. Claude: 15° is what exists. 5° would be 15 lines on each side, offered by proximity, which can be a lot near the node; the angle snap of a dragged heading is already finer with distance (^251). **Decided (user, after ^794):** the angle is the angle of the chord from the node, and a curve that leaves the node at the angle α to its chord turns by **2α** (at the normal line, 90°, it is a half circle, 180°; tested with the automatic heading), so 15° steps of the line are 30° steps of the curve. The extend lines are at 7.5° to 82.5° in steps of 7.5° to either side (before: 15° to 75°), so that the curve turns in steps of 15°. Finer steps only far from the node (^251) stays an idea for later. |

| ^749 | decision | decided (user, review of ^371) | **Operations are not state transitions (user):** an operation is what the user did, with a name (^435); what the data model does as a result is a state transition and can differ each time. | ^751 | decision | assumed (the user asked for Claude's pick; milestone ^309 expands it) | **`join road` instead of `merge road` (user's question, Claude's pick):** the operation in which the road being drawn ends at an existing node or curve. Its outcomes are state transitions (^748): joining a leaf node continues the road, ending at another node or at a curve makes a junction, ending at its own start makes a loop. Graph theory has no single term (*contraction* merges two nodes, nothing standard names this), so *join* is the nearest everyday word. *Merge* stays for two nodes becoming one (a feature, ^248). Replaces the name `merge road` (^550); `close road` is discarded (^751). |

| ^792 | bug | done (milestone ^794, PR #36) | **The heading at a crossing of snap lines depends on the order of the list (found when the user asked which heading wins):** at a snap point where two curves cross, the hit's heading is that of the *first* curve in `by` (`placedHeading` reads `by[0]`), and `by` is the near curves in the order of the suggestion list (the order of the nodes). Scratch test: node A (heading line along x, no heading) crosses node B's normal line (heading 0): with the nodes in the order A, B the snap has **no** heading, in the order B, A it has 0°; two normal lines with 0° and 90° give 0° or 90° depending on the order alone. A line without a heading in front hides the heading of the other. A snap point (a node's points, a radius point) wins over the curves through it, which is intended (a point is more specific). See ^793 for what should happen. **Fixed also (found while checking the milestone):** coinciding lines are one suggestion (^275), which hid the order dependence a second time: the heading line of two nodes that face each other kept the first node's base, and the normal line of two nodes with opposite headings (the two lanes of a road at one place) kept the first node's heading. Now a merged line that gives different headings gives none. |
| ^793 | question | done (milestone ^794, PR #36; the user agreed to 1 to 3: tangent lines give the heading along themselves, a crossing by specificity and agreement, conflict means none) | **Snap suggestions that carry a heading, in general (user: the tangent snap lines do not suggest a heading, they should):** which suggestions give a node placed on them a heading (`placedHeading`, ^253, ^254, ^317, ^318, ^319, ^358; the dragged heading control point has its own, `headingFrom`, ^313, ^301, ^335): a node's points (the node's heading, the opposite one on its other side); a segment's curve and its offsets (the lane's direction, ^317); a node's *normal* line (the node's heading, so lanes stay parallel, ^318); the tick lines (the tangent of the curve); the inflection point; the inflection *normal* line (the tangent), but the inflection *tangent* line gives the direction sideways (+90°); **no heading**: a node's *heading* line (^266, by design: the code says a road drawn on along it keeps its automatic heading, not a sideways one), the bend lines (^269), the extend lines (^473). So: normal-type lines copy the heading of their node, tangent-type lines copy none, and the inflection tangent line is the odd one out. **The user's take:** tangent lines should suggest a heading. **State at the end of ^794, which suggestions give a heading to a node placed or moved onto them:** *lines:* a node's normal line (its heading), its heading line (along it), the tick lines (the tangent), the bisector of a bend (the tangent) and its tangent line (along it), the inflection lines, the middle line of a straight segment (the segment's direction); *points:* the side points of a node, the previous position of a moved node (its own heading); *lanes:* the offset curves and the own curves. **No heading, by design:** the extend lines (^325 to ^340: they say at what angle the segment leaves the node being drawn from; the heading of the new node is then the automatic one, which makes the arc smooth, ^473), a node's own point and the curve of a segment (a node placed there is merged into it), the radius points and the inflection point (a node placed there is inserted into the curve, ^307). **Claude's take (point 3 revised by the user, ^797: the nearer node decides):** (1) a tangent-type line (a node's heading line, the inflection tangent line) gives the heading *along itself*, pointing away from its base, the same rule the dragged heading already uses (`headingFrom`, ^313: one rule for placing and dragging); `Alt` inverts (^301). The inflection tangent line must not point sideways. (2) The bend lines and the extend lines stay without one (they have no meaning as a direction of travel). (3) **A crossing:** the heading comes from the most specific suggestion, the same ranking as the position (points, then the curves of segments and their offsets, then lines); among suggestions of the same rank those that have a heading must all agree (the same direction), then it is that heading, else **none** (position only, the automatic heading): no order dependence (^792), no silent guess, and a line without a heading does not hide one that has. (4) Hint and toast follow what happens (^181, ^319). Needs the user's decision before code. |

| ^802 | bug | done (milestone ^794, PR #36; user, cause found by Claude) | **Sliding along a segment does not snap to the other suggestions, in draw mode (split, insert) and in extend mode (merge) (user):** only moving the pointer further away (still within the snap distance) made the other snap lines work. **Cause:** a second, older mechanism ran before the snap engine: when the pointer was over the curve of a segment (the hover, a hit test of its own with a smaller distance), `snapFor` did not ask the engine at all but returned the hovered place on the curve (`hoveredPlace`, from ^289 and ^296: "a node or a curve that is hovered already is it"), and `snapPreview` left such a hover as it was. **Fix:** over the curve the engine is asked with what is *on the curve* only (where a snap line crosses it, a point on it), so the pointer is pulled to the crossing of a line with the segment, and a click splits or merges there; whatever is beside the curve (a crossing of two lines near it) is not used, because it would take a click off the curve (the e2e scenario of ^145 clicks next to one; it now holds `Ctrl`, ^101). ## Rented ranges

A range of hats whose rows are written in another file instead of here (^392). This file stays the one place that mints hats: a range is reserved here first, the file holds one row per hat of its range (each row starts with `| ^N |`), none missing, none outside the range, and `next:` counts the range. Checked by `src/lib/hats.test.ts`.
